ChannelCreate(\n    std::string target, ChannelArgs args,\n    grpc_channel_stack_type channel_stack_type, Transport* optional_transport) {\n  global_stats().IncrementClientChannelsCreated();\n  // For client channels, canonify target string and add channel arg.\n  // Note: We don't do this for direct channels or lame channels.\n  if (channel_stack_type == GRPC_CLIENT_CHANNEL) {\n    target =\n        CoreConfiguration::Get().resolver_registry().AddDefaultPrefixIfNeeded(\n            target);\n    args = args.Set(GRPC_ARG_SERVER_URI, target);\n  }\n  // Set default authority if needed.\n  if      .value_or(GRPC_ENABLE_CHANNELZ_DEFAULT)) {\n    // Get parameters needed to create the channelz node.\n    const size_t channel_tracer_max_memory = std::max(\n        0, args.GetBool(GRPC_ARG_CHANNELZ_IS_INTERNAL_CHANNEL).value_or(false);\n    // Create the channelz node.\n    std::string channelz_node_target{target.empty() ? \"unknown\" : target};\n    auto channelz_node = MakeRefCounted<channelz::ChannelNode>(\n        channelz_node_target, channel_tracer_max_memory, is_internal_channel);\n    channelz_node->AddTraceEvent(\n        channelz::ChannelTrace::Severity::Info,\n        grpc_slice_from_static_string(\"Channel created\"));\n    // Add channelz node to channel args.\n    // We remove the is_internal_channel arg, since we no longer need it.\n    args = args.Remove(GRPC_ARG_CHANNELZ_IS_INTERNAL_CHANNEL)\n               .SetObject(std::move(channelz_node));\n  }\n  // Add transport to args.\n  if (optional_transport != nullptr) {\n    args = args.SetObject(optional_transport);\n  }\n  // Delegate to appropriate channel impl.\n  if (!args.GetBool(GRPC_ARG_USE_V3_STACK).value_or(false)) {\n    return LegacyChannel::Create(std::move(target), std::move(args),\n                                 channel_stack_type);\n  }\n  switch (channel_stack_type) {\n    case GRPC_CLIENT_CHANNEL:\n      return A push-based mechanism is one where the resolver will\n    41→/// subscribe to updates for a given name, and the name service will\n    42→/// proactively send new data to the resolver whenever the data associated\n    43→/// with the name changes.  A pull-based mechanism is one where the resolver\n    44→/// needs to query the name service again to get updated information (e.g.,\n    45→/// DNS).\n    46→///\n    47→/// Note: All methods with a \"Locked\" suffix must be called from the\n    48→/// work_serializer passed to the constructor.\n    49→class Resolver : public InternallyRefCounted<Resolver> {\n    50→ public:\n    51→  /// Results returned by the resolver.\n    52→  struct Result {\n    53→    /// A list of endpoints, each with one or more addresses, or an error.\n    54→    absl::StatusOr<EndpointAddressesList> addresses;\n    55→    /// A service config, or an error.\n    56→    absl::StatusOr<RefCountedPtr<ServiceConfig>> service_config = nullptr;\n    57→    /// An optional human-readable note describing context about the resolution,\n    58→    /// to be passed along to the LB policy for inclusion in RPC failure status\n    59→    /// messages in cases where neither \\a addresses nor \\a service_config\n    60→    /// has a non-OK status. A push-based mechanism is one where the resolver will\n/// subscribe to updates for a given name, and the name service will\n/// proactively send new data to the resolver whenever the data associated\n/// with the name changes.  A pull-based mechanism is one where the resolver\n/// needs to query the name service again to get updated information (e.g.,\n/// DNS).\n///\n/// Note: All methods with a \"Locked\" suffix must be called from the\n/// work_serializer passed to the constructor.\nclass Resolver : public InternallyRefCounted<Resolver> {\n public:\n  /// Results returned by the resolver.\n  struct Result {\n    /// A list of endpoints, each with one or more addresses, or an error.\n    absl::StatusOr<EndpointAddressesList> addresses;\n    /// A service config, or an error.\n    absl::StatusOr<RefCountedPtr<ServiceConfig>> service_config = nullptr;\n    /// An optional human-readable note describing context about the resolution,\n    /// to be passed along to the LB policy for inclusion in RPC failure status\n    /// messages in cases where neither \\a addresses nor \\a service_config\n    /// has a non-OK status.  For example, a resolver that returns an empty\n    /// address list but a valid service config may set to this to something\n    /// like \"no DNS entries found for <name>\".\n    std::string resolution_note;\n    // TODO(roth): Before making this a public API, figure out a way to\n    // avoid exposing channel args this way.\n    ChannelArgs args;\n    // If non-null, this callback will be invoked when the LB policy has\n    // processed the result.  The status value passed to the callback\n    // indicates whether the LB policy accepted the update.  For polling\n    // resolvers, if the reported status is non-OK, then the resolver\n    // should put itself into backoff to retry the resolution later.\n    // The resolver impl must not call ResultHandler::ReportResult()\n    // again until after this callback has been invoked.\n    // The callback will be invoked within the channel's WorkSerializer.\n    // It may or may not be invoked before ResultHandler::ReportResult()\n    // returns, which is why it's a separate callback.\n    std::function<void(absl::Status)> result_health_callback;\n  };\n\n  /// A proxy object used by the resolver to return results to the\n  /// client channel.\n  class ResultHandler {\n   public:\n    virtual ~ResultHandler() {}\n\n    /// Reports a result to the channel.\n    virtual void ReportResult(Result result) = 0;  // NOLINT\n  };\n\n  // Not copyable nor movable.\n  Resolver(const Resolver&) = delete;\n  Resolver& operator=(const Resolver&) = delete;\n  ~Resolver() override = default;\n\n  /// Starts resolving.\n  virtual void StartLocked() = 0;\n\n  /// Asks the resolver to obtain an updated resolver result, if\n  /// applicable.\n  ///\n  /// This is useful for pull-based implementations to decide when to\n  /// re-resolve.  However, the implementation is not required to\n  /// re-resolve immediately upon receiving this call; it may instead\n  /// elect to delay based on some configured minimum time between\n  /// queries, to avoid hammering the name service with queries.\n  ///\n  /// For push-based implementations, this may be a no-op.\n  ///\n  /// Note: Implementations must not invoke any method on the\n  /// ResultHandler from within this call.\n  virtual void RequestReresolutionLocked() {}\n\n  /// Resets the re-resolution backoff, if any.\n  /// This needs to be implemented only by pull-based implementations;\n  /// for push-based implementations, it will be a no-op.\n  virtual void ResetBackoffLocked() {}\n\n  // Note: This must be invoked while holding the work_serializer.\n  void Orphan() override {\n    ShutdownLocked();\n    Unref();\n  }\n\n protected:\n  Resolver();\n\n  /// Shuts down the resolver.\n  virtual void ShutdownLocked() = 0;\n};\n\n}  // namespace grpc_core\n\n#endif  // GRPC_SRC_CORE_RESOLVER_RESOLVER_H\n","numLines":134,"startLine":1,"totalLines":134}}}

pipeline is implemented across multiple components in src/core/:\\n\\n**Channel Creation Pipeline:**\\nThe entry point is ChannelCreate() in src/core/lib/surface/channel_create.cc, which creates a Channel instance (defined in src/core/lib/surface/channel.h). For client channels with the V3 stack enabled, it delegates to ClientChannel::Create() (src/core/client_channel/client_channel.h), which is the main client-side channel implementation that integrates load balancing and connectivity management.\\n\\n**Subchannel Allocation Logic:**\\nSubchannel allocation is managed through the SubchannelPoolInterface (src/core/client_channel/subchannel_pool_interface.h) with two implementations: GlobalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/global_subchannel_pool.h) for shared subchannel pooling and LocalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/local_subchannel_pool.h) for per-channel pools. The Subchannel class itself is defined in src/core/client_channel/subchannel.h and implements the actual connection management to individual addresses.\\n\\n**Load Balancing Policies:**\\nThe load balancing pipeline is built on the LoadBalancingPolicy base class (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy.h). The two fundamental policy implementations are:\\n- RoundRobin: src/core/load_balancing/round_robin/round_robin.cc, which distributes requests across healthy subchannels\\n- PickFirst: src/core/load_balancing/pick_first/pick_first.cc, which connects to the first available subchannel\\n\\nBoth policies extend from LoadBalancingPolicy and use EndpointList (src/core/load_balancing/endpoint_list.h) to manage endpoint state. Policies are registered via LoadBalancingPolicyRegistry (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_registry.h) and created through LoadBalancingPolicyFactory (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_factory.h). The ChildPolicyHandler (src/core/load_balancing/child_policy_handler.h) enables composable policy hierarchies.\\n\\n**Name Resolution Interface:**\\nThe name resolution system uses the Resolver interface (src/core/resolver/resolver.h) for push/pull-based resolution. ResolverFactory (src/core/resolver/resolver_factory.h) is the abstract factory for creating resolver instances. The ResolverRegistry (src/core/resolver/resolver_registry.h) manages registered factory implementations. A concrete example is NativeClientChannelDNSResolver (src/core/resolver/dns/native/dns_resolver.cc), which implements DNS resolution using the native resolver.\\n\\n**Connectivity State Machine:**\\nConnectivity state management is implemented through ConnectivityStateTracker (src/core/lib/transport/connectivity_state.h), which tracks channel/subchannel states (IDLE, CONNECTING, READY, TRANSIENT_FAILURE, SHUTDOWN) and notifies watchers of state transitions. The interface supports both synchronous (ConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) and asynchronous (AsyncConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) notification patterns.\"\n}\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":7,"cache_creation_input_tokens":890,"cache_read_input_tokens":48807,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":890,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"678ba023-9ab2-430d-bdfb-47b7b52ab94f","uuid":"331cff3d-cd29-40c0-864e-b3421ac77d1e"}

pipeline is implemented across multiple components in src/core/:\\n\\n**Channel Creation Pipeline:**\\nThe entry point is ChannelCreate() in src/core/lib/surface/channel_create.cc, which creates a Channel instance (defined in src/core/lib/surface/channel.h). For client channels with the V3 stack enabled, it delegates to ClientChannel::Create() (src/core/client_channel/client_channel.h), which is the main client-side channel implementation that integrates load balancing and connectivity management.\\n\\n**Subchannel Allocation Logic:**\\nSubchannel allocation is managed through the SubchannelPoolInterface (src/core/client_channel/subchannel_pool_interface.h) with two implementations: GlobalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/global_subchannel_pool.h) for shared subchannel pooling and LocalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/local_subchannel_pool.h) for per-channel pools. The Subchannel class itself is defined in src/core/client_channel/subchannel.h and implements the actual connection management to individual addresses.\\n\\n**Load Balancing Policies:**\\nThe load balancing pipeline is built on the LoadBalancingPolicy base class (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy.h). The two fundamental policy implementations are:\\n- RoundRobin: src/core/load_balancing/round_robin/round_robin.cc, which distributes requests across healthy subchannels\\n- PickFirst: src/core/load_balancing/pick_first/pick_first.cc, which connects to the first available subchannel\\n\\nBoth policies extend from LoadBalancingPolicy and use EndpointList (src/core/load_balancing/endpoint_list.h) to manage endpoint state. Policies are registered via LoadBalancingPolicyRegistry (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_registry.h) and created through LoadBalancingPolicyFactory (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_factory.h). The ChildPolicyHandler (src/core/load_balancing/child_policy_handler.h) enables composable policy hierarchies.\\n\\n**Name Resolution Interface:**\\nThe name resolution system uses the Resolver interface (src/core/resolver/resolver.h) for push/pull-based resolution. ResolverFactory (src/core/resolver/resolver_factory.h) is the abstract factory for creating resolver instances. The ResolverRegistry (src/core/resolver/resolver_registry.h) manages registered factory implementations. A concrete example is NativeClientChannelDNSResolver (src/core/resolver/dns/native/dns_resolver.cc), which implements DNS resolution using the native resolver.\\n\\n**Connectivity State Machine:**\\nConnectivity state management is implemented through ConnectivityStateTracker (src/core/lib/transport/connectivity_state.h), which tracks channel/subchannel states (IDLE, CONNECTING, READY, TRANSIENT_FAILURE, SHUTDOWN) and notifies watchers of state transitions. The interface supports both synchronous (ConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) and asynchronous (AsyncConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) notification patterns.\"\n}\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

pipeline is implemented across multiple components in src/core/:\\n\\n**Channel Creation Pipeline:**\\nThe entry point is ChannelCreate() in src/core/lib/surface/channel_create.cc, which creates a Channel instance (defined in src/core/lib/surface/channel.h). For client channels with the V3 stack enabled, it delegates to ClientChannel::Create() (src/core/client_channel/client_channel.h), which is the main client-side channel implementation that integrates load balancing and connectivity management.\\n\\n**Subchannel Allocation Logic:**\\nSubchannel allocation is managed through the SubchannelPoolInterface (src/core/client_channel/subchannel_pool_interface.h) with two implementations: GlobalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/global_subchannel_pool.h) for shared subchannel pooling and LocalSubchannelPool (src/core/client_channel/local_subchannel_pool.h) for per-channel pools. The Subchannel class itself is defined in src/core/client_channel/subchannel.h and implements the actual connection management to individual addresses.\\n\\n**Load Balancing Policies:**\\nThe load balancing pipeline is built on the LoadBalancingPolicy base class (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy.h). The two fundamental policy implementations are:\\n- RoundRobin: src/core/load_balancing/round_robin/round_robin.cc, which distributes requests across healthy subchannels\\n- PickFirst: src/core/load_balancing/pick_first/pick_first.cc, which connects to the first available subchannel\\n\\nBoth policies extend from LoadBalancingPolicy and use EndpointList (src/core/load_balancing/endpoint_list.h) to manage endpoint state. Policies are registered via LoadBalancingPolicyRegistry (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_registry.h) and created through LoadBalancingPolicyFactory (src/core/load_balancing/lb_policy_factory.h). The ChildPolicyHandler (src/core/load_balancing/child_policy_handler.h) enables composable policy hierarchies.\\n\\n**Name Resolution Interface:**\\nThe name resolution system uses the Resolver interface (src/core/resolver/resolver.h) for push/pull-based resolution. ResolverFactory (src/core/resolver/resolver_factory.h) is the abstract factory for creating resolver instances. The ResolverRegistry (src/core/resolver/resolver_registry.h) manages registered factory implementations. A concrete example is NativeClientChannelDNSResolver (src/core/resolver/dns/native/dns_resolver.cc), which implements DNS resolution using the native resolver.\\n\\n**Connectivity State Machine:**\\nConnectivity state management is implemented through ConnectivityStateTracker (src/core/lib/transport/connectivity_state.h), which tracks channel/subchannel states (IDLE, CONNECTING, READY, TRANSIENT_FAILURE, SHUTDOWN) and notifies watchers of state transitions. The interface supports both synchronous (ConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) and asynchronous (AsyncConnectivityStateWatcherInterface) notification patterns.\"\n}\n","numLines":66,"startLine":1,"totalLines":66}}}
